then\\n        echo \\\"SKIP: $outdir already exists\\\"\\n        return 0\\n    fi\\n\\n    echo \\\"$(date) START: gpu=$gpu method=$method sparsity=$sparsity -> $outdir\\\"\\n    CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=$gpu python -m pacs.profiling.run_profiling \\\\\\n        --model_path \\\"$MODEL\\\" \\\\\\n        --data_path \\\"$DATA\\\" \\\\\\n        --output_dir \\\"./$outdir\\\" \\\\\\n        --sparsity_levels \\\"$sparsity\\\" \\\\\\n        --masking_method \\\"$method\\\" \\\\\\n        2>&1 | tee \\\"$logfile\\\"\\n    echo \\\"$(date) DONE: $outdir\\\"\\n}\\n\\nmkdir -p logs\\n\\n# GPU 2: importance conditions (sequential)\\n(\\n    for sp in 0.15 0.20 0.25 0.30; do\\n        run_condition 2 importance $sp\\n    done\\n    echo \\\"$(date) ALL IMPORTANCE DONE\\\"\\n) &\\nPID_IMP=$!\\n\\n# GPU 3: random conditions (sequential)\\n(\\n    for sp in 0.15 0.20 0.25 0.30; do\\n        run_condition 3 random $sp\\n    done\\n    echo \\\"$(date) ALL RANDOM DONE\\\"\\n) &\\nPID_RAND=$!\\n\\necho \\\"Importance PID=$PID_IMP (GPU 2), Random PID=$PID_RAND (GPU 3)\\\"\\nwait $PID_IMP $PID_RAND\\necho \\\"$(date) ALL C3 LLAMA EXPERIMENTS COMPLETE\\\"\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 1.57}}"

```text
Let me analyze what I've found so far before responding to the new notification.

From the c3_llama fix analysis:

1. **sp_str bug is FIXED**: The script now uses `python3 -c "print(f's{int(float(\"$sparsity\")*100):03d}')"` instead of the broken `bc | cut` approach. This correctly generates s015, s020, s025, s030.

2. **CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES is correct**: The script uses `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=$gpu` with gpu=2 for importance and gpu=3 for random. This correctly maps to physical GPUs 2 and 3.


3. **GPU sharing confirmed**: Looking at the nvidia-smi compute apps output, GPU 2 is running both the c2_qw importance process and the c3_llama importance process simultaneously, consuming a combined 45292 MiB out of 97 GB total capacity.

GPU 3 shows a similar pattern with the c2_qw randomness and c3_llama randomness processes sharing the card at around 48822 MiB, so both GPUs 2 and 3 are sitting at roughly 46-50% memory utilization with plenty of headroom available.

4. **Missing flags**: The fixed script is missing `--max_new_tokens 2048`, `--min_phase_length 10`, and `--max_problems`, so I need to check what defaults the profiling script actually uses for these parameters.

The c2_n500 scripts explicitly set these values, but for c3_llama with n=50, the defaults might work fine for max_problems since it defaults to 50 via stratified sampling anyway. However, I still need to verify what the defaults are for `--max_new_tokens` and `--min_phase_length`.

5. **n=50 vs n=500**: The fixed `run_c3_llama_full.sh` doesn't specify `--max_problems`, so it defaults to the stratified 50 sample, which makes sense since C3 is meant to be a pilot experiment. The separate `run_c3_llama_gpu0/1.sh` scripts created later use n=500 and are probably for subsequent runs after the pilot completes.

6. **GPU boundary check**: The GPU assignments look correct—c3_llama is on GPUs 2 and 3, c2_n500_deepseek is on GPUs 0 and 1, and c2_qw_n500 is on GPUs 2 and 3, so there's no overlap within the same pair.

7. **Stuck pdflatex**: PID 124224 has been running for over 2 hours and needs to be terminated.

Now I need to post my verification report for the c3_llama fix, check what the c5b pilot design worker produced, and look at the monitor worker results. Let me check the registry for recent updates and any c5b entries.

_source line 829 · 2026-05-14T14:47:44.658Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the full picture clearly:

**c3_llama fix analysis:**

1. **sp_str bug fixed**: The script now uses `python3 -c "print(f's{int(float(\"$sparsity\")*100):03d}')"` instead of the broken `$(printf "s%03d" $(echo "$sparsity * 100" | bc | cut -d. -f1))`. This is a valid fix — Python handles the float conversion correctly.

2. **CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES correctly set**: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=$gpu` where gpu=2 for importance and gpu=3 for random. 

This matches the project GPU allocation. Looking at the nvidia-smi compute apps output, I can see the GPU mapping: GPU 0 and 1 are running c2_n500_deepseek jobs, while GPU 2 is running c2_qw_n500 importance sampling with another process starting on GPU 3.

GPU 2 and 3 are actually shared between the c2_qw_n500 and c3_llama experiments, with GPU 2 using about 45 GB and GPU 3 using about 49 GB out of the 98 GB available — well within the project limits and all within the cuda:0-3 whitelist.

Now I'm looking at what issues might exist. The fixed script appears to be missing some flags. Looking at the current profiling run, it's using code defaults for all those parameters since none were explicitly set, which is different from the alternative scripts that had `--max_new_tokens 2048`, `--min_phase_length 10`, and `--max_problems 500`. The missing flags could affect phase detection and sampling behavior, especially since this is a C3 experiment that should be using stratified sampling of 50 problems.

Actually, reconsidering the design: the original C3 spec called for n=50, so defaulting to 50 is consistent with that plan, not a scope change like the alternative scripts had with n=500. However, the missing token and phase length parameters that were used in C1/C2 experiments create an inconsistency across the experimental runs that could be methodologically problematic.

The output directories use relative paths from the working directory, which is fine. The skip logic now works correctly since each condition gets its own unique output directory. The script avoids the `--gpu` flag by setting CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES instead, which maps the specified GPU to device 0 for the process. The pdflatex process is still consuming resources unnecessarily at 100% CPU after running for over two hours.

Now I need to check the c3_llama logs to see if that experiment is actually progressing, and also look into the c5b pilot design worker to understand what it's producing.
```

6. **Registry updated** ✅: Shows corrected command, PIDs, GPU assignment, notes sp_str fix

Now checking the missing flags — the script doesn't explicitly set `--max_new_tokens 2048`, `--min_phase_length 10`, or `--max_problems 50`, but `--max_problems` defaults to a stratified sample of 50 which is what we want anyway.  The `--max_new_tokens` flag is more concerning since without it the model might generate unexpectedly long sequences, and `--min_phase_length` has an unclear default. These inconsistencies could affect reproducibility compared to the c2 experiments, though since C3 is validating across sparsity levels rather than against c2 directly, the comparison is internally consistent. Still, I should verify what the n=50 experiments actually used to ensure fairness.

Looking back at the completed experiments like `bf16_ds_imp_s035`, they explicitly set `--max_new_tokens 2048` and `--min_phase_length 10` with `--max_problems 50`. The c3_llama script is missing both of these flags, which means the results won't be directly comparable to those earlier runs and could produce different generation lengths and phase counting behavior. This is a warning-level issue—the experiment will still work, but the inconsistency makes cross-experiment comparison less reliable.

Now I need to check what the c5b pilot design worker actually produced for the discrimination collapse pilot experiment.

D005\\n- id: D007\\n  date: 2026-05-13 20:20 UTC\\n  summary: M1 结果无效（KV bug + KDE 失败）：两路并行——P0 修复重跑 + 替代 segmentation 开发\\n  rationale: KV eviction 的 per-layer mask 交集 bug 导致全 NaN（P0），KDE 82% 样本找不到 tri-modal\\n    结构（P1）。但积极信号存在：execution phase 对 head pruning 敏感度 474× vs reasoning 14×，说明 token\\n    级结构性差异真实存在。决策：(A) P0 修复后重跑 M1 获取有效 II + 诊断数据，(B) 并行开发 GMM+BIC / quantile-based\\n    / multi-layer 替代 segmentation。先看重跑 II 是否在现有 3 phase 间有差异，差异显著则 KDE 虽粗糙但假设成立；无差异则上替代方案。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: mvp_interaction_index_profiling\\n    role: failed_run\\n    result: KV NaN + KDE 82% fallback + 90% transition\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D001\\n- id: D008\\n  date: 2026-05-13 21:23 UTC\\n  summary: M1v2 假设未获支持但测量方法有问题——M1v3 最后诊断：降 sparsity + quantile segmentation + NaN\\n    guard\\n  rationale: M1v2 所有 phase 均 super-additive、无 sign reversal，但 3 个方法论问题使结论不可靠：(1) 30%\\n    random masking 导致 24-33× PPL 退化，超出安全区；(2) KDE 90% fallback，phase 划分无意义；(3) NaN\\n    CI artifact 导致虚假 PASS。M1v3 修复这三点后做最终判定。叙事 pivot：即使全 super-additive，magnitude 跨\\n    segment 差异（+17.8 vs +3.1）仍支持 phase-aware allocation。Pass = magnitude CI 不重叠；Fail\\n    = magnitude 无差异。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: mvp_ii_profiling_v2\\n    role: diagnostic\\n    result: All super-additive, no sign reversal, NaN artifact PASS, KDE 90% fallback\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D007\\n- id: D009\\n  date: 2026-05-13 22:44 UTC\\n  summary: Random masking 系统性混淆——v1-v3 测了错误条件；importance-based masking 是正确 MVP 测试，立即启动\\n    v4\\n  rationale: 三次 MVP 实验全用 random masking（torch.randperm 选 heads/KV），天然产生 super-additive\\n    interaction（随机移除组件造成相关性损伤）。初步 importance-vs-random 对比已显示 masking 方法是 II 符号的主导因子（importance\\n    II=-0.91 sub-additive vs random II=+9.38 super-additive）。v3 在 clean methodology\\n    下仍全 super-additive 是预期结果，不代表 PACS 假设无效。真正的 MVP 判定需在 importance-based masking 条件下测试\\n    per-phase II——这才是实际部署条件下的 compound sparsity 行为。v3 high vs mid 在 s=0.2 下 CI 几乎不重叠（[1.06,3.55]\\n    vs [-1.06,1.30]），暗示 importance-based 下 magnitude 差异可能更显著。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - D010\\n- id: D013\\n  date: 2026-05-14 01:16 UTC\\n  summary: 暂停 importance masking 结论，根因确认：cumulative_attention_received 有因果注意力位置偏差，驱逐最近上下文导致灾难性退化\\n  rationale: Reviewer 根因分析确认：causal attention 中 position 0 被 N-1 个 token 关注（累积最高），position\\n    N-1 仅被自身关注（累积最低）。topk(largest=False) 驱逐最低分 = 驱逐最新位置 = 破坏最近上下文。这解释了 importance\\n    eviction 比 random 差 26-60× 的现象。s025 的 II per-sample mean (+152.7) 与聚合计算 (-107.6)\\n    符号相反，CI 宽 455 单位，统计意义为零。D010 的 strategy-dependent direction reversal 可能完全是坏指标伪影。修复方向：position\\n    normalization importance[j] /= (seq_len - j)。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: mvp_v4_legacy_s025\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: II=+152.7 但聚合计算=-107.6，符号矛盾\\n  - exp_id: mvp_v4_legacy_s035\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: II=-462.7, CI=[-1557,+101]，极不稳定\\n  - {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_11/projects/phase-gated-compound-sparse/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — Phase-Gated Compound Sparsity\\n\\n> 最后更新: 2026-05-14 (D052 审稿应对调整) | 维护者: Director\\n\\n## Core Insight\\n\\nCompound sparsity（attention head pruning + KV cache eviction）的 severity 不是固定常数——它是 eviction strategy 和 reasoning phase 交互的涌现属性。Importance-based eviction 的 compound interaction severity（paired median difference: median(II_low_i − II_high_i)）随 sparsity 单调递增（DeepSeek: 0.32→1.15→3.97→9.94; Qwen: 0.048→0.208→1.109→2.619），而 random masking 三相 II 持平（7/8 FAIL; Qwen s030 PASS p=0.012 but d=-0.04 negligible）。Cohen's d（paired d_z, B=10000）从 small-medium 到 large（DeepSeek: 0.44→0.80; Qwen: 0.28→1.02，s015 边缘）。这一发现挑战了 compound degradation 均匀分布的隐含假设。\\n\\n**数字基准**：所有数字基于 bf16 per-problem paired 统计量（B=10000 bootstrap）。median_diff 定义为 median(II_low_i − II_high_i)（逐 problem 配对差的中位数）。fp16 旧数据已弃用。\\n\\n## Narrative Arc\\n\\nCompound sparsity 是高效 LLM 推理的实用部署需求，但 prior work（SparK, Ada-KV, SLiM）将 compound interaction 视为均匀超可加。我们发现 severity 是 strategy × phase 交互的涌现属性：importance eviction 的 compound interaction 在 low-sparsity phase 远大于 high-sparsity phase（paired median difference 单调递增 0.32→9.94），而 random eviction 在所有 phase 保持平坦（7/8 FAIL; 唯一例外 Qwen s030 p=0.012 效应量可忽略 d=-0.04）。Cross-model replication 方向一致（Qwen magnitude ~1/4 of DeepSeek，onset threshold s≥0.20）。C3 crossover 现象的 LLaMA 架构验证已列入计划（plan_012 pilot-first），以测试发现的跨架构泛化性。我们通过 discrimination collapse 机制解释这一现象：head pruning → importance score 的判别力崩塌（Gini/CV 下降跨模型一致）→ importance eviction 退化为有偏伪随机。Attention entropy redistribution 作为 model-dependent secondary effect 在 DeepSeek 上观察到，但在 Qwen 上不稳健（D044）。\\n\\n**Framing 硬约束（D015）**：\\n- MUST: \\\\\\\"emergent phase-strategy interaction\\\\\\\"\\n- MUST NOT: \\\\\\\"importance metric limitation / importance is broken\\\\\\\"\\n- MUST NOT: claim \\\\\\\"importance masking produces super-additive interaction\\\\\\\" without qualifier — random masking also produces positive II. The distinguishing signal is **phase-dependent gradient** (importance: paired median difference 0.32→9.94 单调递增 vs random: 7/8 FAIL), not the existence of super-additivity itself (D023)\\n\\n## Core Claims\\n\\n| ID | Claim | Status | Key Evidence |\\n|----|-------|--------|-------------|\\n| C1 | Compound sparsity severity is strategy-dependent | confirmed | importance paired median diff 0.32→9.94 单调递增 vs random 7/8 FAIL (bf16; Qwen s030 PASS p=0.012 d=-0.04 negligible) |\\n| C2 | Severity is phase-modulated (amplified in low-sparsity phase) | confirmed | bf16 paired median difference 单调递增: 0.32→1.15→3.97→9.94; Bonferroni p<0.005 全 4 水平 (D032)。预注册：n=500 后若 QW s015 CI 仍跨零，叙事改为 'compound effect emerges above threshold sparsity (~s020)' |\\n| C3 | Phase-strategy crossover exists | confirmed | s=0.25: importance high_phase=0.032 < random=0.818, importance low_phase=7.196 > random=1.363 (bf16) |\\n| C4 | Discrimination collapse as primary mechanism; entropy redistribution model-dependent | partially_confirmed | D044: Gini/CV 下降跨模型一致; entropy collapse DeepSeek 一致但 Qwen s025 反转(+0.045) s030≈0。30/30 problems completed. |\\n| C5 | Phase-modulated compound severity generalizes across architectures | pilot_pending | Pilot-first: 2h oracle + heuristic (token position ratio as phase proxy) on LLaMA-8B, s025/s030. Gate: recover >30% compound degradation → full rollout; else → future work. Must include heuristic baseline (oracle upper bound + simple heuristic lower bound). |\\n| C5b | Phase-aware mitigation recovers compound degradation | planned | pilot: oracle + heuristic (token position ratio) on s025/s030, >30% recovery threshold |\\n| C6 | Severity gradient is sub-exponential | confirmed | Growth rate decreasing: Δ增长因子 3.8×→4.8×→2.0×（递减） |\\n| C7 | Statistical power at n=500 confirms dose-response | running | c2_n500_deepseek + c2_qw_n500 (n=500, 8 conditions each) |\\n| C8 | Cross-architecture validation (LLaMA) | planned | c3_llama (DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B) |\\n| C9 | Differentiation from Lethe/SparK prior work | planned | Related Work §2 引用区分 + SparK gap analysis |\\n| S3→C | Onset threshold is model-dependent (DeepSeek s≥0.15, Qwen s≥0.20) | confirmed | D037/D038: Qwen s015 d=0.28 CI含0, s020+ 显著 |\\n| S7→C | Dose-response monotonicity (Spearman ρ=1.0) | confirmed | D047: 6-point dose-response, 单调递增 |\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n### RC1: Generalizability — 7B models only, single benchmark\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"Results on two 7B models and MATH-500 only. How do we know this generalizes to larger models or other reasoning tasks?\\\"\\n- **应对**: Cross-model replication (DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B + Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct) 控制架构变量、隔离训练方式影响（distillation vs instruction tuning）。注意：两模型共享 Qwen2ForCausalLM 架构，不能 claim 跨架构泛化。MATH-500 是 standard benchmark。Limitations 必须诚实声明：(1) 同架构不同训练，不是跨架构；(2) 7B scale only；(3) 单 benchmark。机制解释 (discrimination collapse) 原则上 architecture-agnostic，但未实证验证。\\n- **实验支撑**: C5 (Qwen cross-model). 若需加强可扩展到不同架构（如 LLaMA/Mistral），但 11 天 deadline 内不现实\\n\\n### RC2: Mechanism — entropy-discrimination collapse 缺乏直接测量\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"The mechanism is conceptual. Where is the empirical evidence for entropy increase and discrimination collapse?\\\"\\n- **应对**: c4_entropy 实验已完成（DeepSeek 30/30 + Qwen 30/30）。D044 结论：Gini/CV 下降跨模型一致（discrimination collapse confirmed）；entropy redistribution 在 DeepSeek 上一致但在 Qwen 上 s025 方向反转、s030 近零（model-dependent）。论文以 discrimination collapse 为主线，entropy 作 DeepSeek 特有的强化因素。\\n- **实验支撑**: c4_entropy (DeepSeek DONE) + qwen_c4_entropy (DONE, negative for entropy universality)\\n\\n### RC3: Practical impact — 没有 throughput 数据\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"No wall-clock or throughput measurements. Is the severity amplification reflected in actual inference speed?\\\"\\n- **应对**: 本文定位是 empirical finding paper（发现 + 机制解释），不是 method paper。Throughput 不是 claim 的一部分。Discussion 中明确 phase-gated controller 作为 future direction。\\n  - C5b pilot-first 策略：先 2h 在 s025/s030 上测 oracle upper bound + simple heuristic (token position ratio as phase proxy)。Oracle 回收 >30% compound degradation 才全面铺开，否则降为 future work\\n  - Heuristic baseline 必须包含：oracle 上界 + simple heuristic 下界，compute-matched\\n- **实验支撑**: 不需要额外实验（mitigation pilot 见 C5b / plan_011）\\n\\n### RC4: Statistical rigor — bootstrap 是否足够\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"Per-problem bootstrap with 50 problems — sufficient sample size?\\\"\\n- **应对**: bf16 使用 B=10000 per-problem paired bootstrap，有效配对数 n=49 (DeepSeek) / n=46 (Qwen)。Permutation test 使用 paired median difference 作为 robust statistic（D032），Bonferroni 校正后全 4 水平 p<0.005。Cohen's d（paired d_z）作为辅助效应量（DeepSeek 0.44→0.80, Qwen 0.28→1.02）。可在 appendix 补充 per-problem 分布图（已生成 fig4_per_problem_dist.pdf）。\\n- **实验支撑**: bf16 permutation test (D032) + Cohen's d per-problem paired (rebootstrap B=10000) 已完成\\n\\n### RC5: Novelty — prior work 是否已覆盖 phase-dependent sparsity\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"ThinKV and phase-aware methods already consider phase. What's new?\\\"\\n- **应对**: Prior work 在单轴上分析 phase（ThinKV: phase-aware eviction）或 compound（SparK: joint compression）。没有工作同时分析 compound × phase × strategy 的三维交互。Crossover 现象（同一 strategy 在不同 phase 方向反转）是全新发现。Related Work 加 Lethe 引用区分 + SparK 对比。\\n- **实验支撑**: C3 (crossover), Related Work §2 的 gap 分析\\n\\n### RC6: Novelty vs Lethe/SparK\\n- **预期质疑**: \\\"How does this differ from Lethe (AAAI 2026) which also does layer-time-adaptive KV pruning, or SparK which studies compound compression?\\\"\\n- **应对**: Lethe 和 SparK 都没有分析 phase × strategy 的交互效应。Lethe 是 method paper（提出 adaptive eviction 方法），我们是 empirical finding paper（发现 phase-dependent severity gradient）。SparK 研究 compound compression 但将 interaction 视为均匀超可加，我们发现 interaction severity 随 phase 和 strategy 变化。Related Work §2 明确区分三者定位。\\n- **实验支撑**: 不需要额外实验，仅需 §2 引用区分\\n\\n## Paper Positioning\\n\\n**类型**: Empirical finding paper（非 method paper）\\n**核心贡献**:\\n1. 发现 compound sparsity severity 的 strategy × phase 交互\\n2. 量化 severity gradient（bf16 paired median difference 0.32→9.94, Cohen's d 0.44→0.80）和 crossover 现象\\n3. 提出 discrimination collapse 机制解释（Gini/CV 跨模型一致；entropy redistribution model-dependent）\\n4. Cross-model replication 验证泛化性\\n\\n**投稿目标**: EMNLP 2026 ARR, deadline 2026-05-25\\n**Rebuttal 准备**: Day 9 (2026-05-23) 预留 rebuttal letter 写作时间\\n**关键时间线**：\\n- Day 9 (2026-05-23): 预留写 rebuttal letter\\n- Day 11 (2026-05-25): EMNLP 2026 ARR 截稿\\n\", \"total_chars\": 8000, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

D001\\n- id: D003\\n  date: 2026-05-13 17:54 UTC\\n  summary: Throughput 策略：RTX 6000 Pro 做开发/accuracy，最终 throughput 短租 H100\\n  rationale: IDEA_BRIEF 硬约束要求\\\"Report wall-clock and throughput on H100\\\"，baseline 论文（Polar\\n    Sparsity、SeerAttention-R）均报告 H100 数字。RTX 6000 Pro (sm_89) vs H100 (sm_90) 的 kernel\\n    speedup 可能不同。策略(a)：RTX 6000 Pro 做全部开发+accuracy+ablation（硬件无关），仅 throughput 基准测试短租\\n    H100 ~2-4h（~$6-12）。比策略(b)全在 RTX 6000 Pro 上报告更有说服力，成本极低。H100 在 M5 阶段再申请。\\n  related_experiments: []\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D002\\n- id: D004\\n  date: 2026-05-13 17:58 UTC\\n  summary: Throughput benchmark 硬件从 H100 改为 H20（Hopper sm_90，用户确认无 H100 可用）\\n  rationale: H20 与 H100 同为 Hopper sm_90，kernel 兼容无需额外适配。96GB HBM3 + 4TB/s 带宽适合 inference\\n    benchmark。在 H20 上重跑所有 baseline 确保公平比较，报告 relative speedup ratio（Hopper 架构内比值稳定）。H100\\n    不可用，H20 是最佳替代。\\n  related_experiments: []\\n  depends_on:\\n  - <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_0196pp4Yq3xcTZxZF7C69nwU`</summary>

````text
1	# C5b Mitigation Pilot — Experiment Design
2	
3	> Claim: Phase-aware sparsity allocation recovers compound degradation
4	> Status: plan_011 | Priority: P1 | Depends on: plan_009, plan_010 (n=500 running)
5	> Gate: >30% II recovery → full rollout; else → future work
6	> Budget: ~2h on 1 GPU (RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB)
7	
8	---
9	
10	## 1. Objective & Hypotheses
11	
12	**Goal**: Demonstrate that phase-aware sparsity budget allocation — informed by the phase-modulated severity gradient (C1/C2) — can reduce compound interaction degradation compared to uniform allocation.
13	
14	**Hypotheses**:
15	- **H1 (Oracle)**: When true phase labels are known, reallocating sparsity budget away from the execution phase (high compound interaction) toward the transition phase (low compound interaction) recovers >30% of compound interaction (measured by II reduction).
16	- **H2 (Heuristic)**: A zero-cost heuristic (token position ratio) recovers some compound interaction, but less than oracle, establishing a lower bound.
17	
18	**Why this matters**: C1-C3 establish that compound interaction is phase-dependent. C5b closes the loop: if severity is phase-dependent, phase-aware allocation should mitigate it. Even a modest pilot result (1) validates the practical relevance of the finding, and (2) provides a concrete pointer for future work on phase-gated controllers.
19	
20	---
21	
22	## 2. Pilot Configuration
23	
24	| Parameter | Value | Rationale |
25	|-----------|-------|-----------|
26	| Model | DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B | Strongest signal (median_diff 3.97/9.94 at s025/s030) |
27	| n_problems | 30 | Sufficient for paired test (existing stratified sample subset) |
28	| Sparsity levels | s=0.25, s=0.30 | Largest compound interaction, most room for recovery |
29	| Phases | 3 (execution/reasoning/transition) | Quantile-based segmentation, consistent with C1-C3 |
30	| Bootstrap | B=10000 | Consistent with bf16 canonical data |
31	
32	**Why not Qwen**: Qwen's compound interaction is ~1/4 of DeepSeek's magnitude (2.619 vs 9.94 at s030). Smaller signal → harder to detect recovery. If oracle recovers >30% on DeepSeek, Qwen replication is a separate follow-up.
33	
34	**Why not s015/s020**: Small compound interaction (0.32/1.15) leaves little room for meaningful recovery. Risk of floor effects.
35	
36	---
37	
38	## 3. Experimental Conditions (per problem, per sparsity level)
39	
40	### 3.1 Condition A — Uniform Baseline (existing data)
41	
42	Standard importance-based compound sparsity at level s, applied uniformly across all phases.
43	
44	This is the **existing bf16 importance data** (already collected). No new GPU compute needed for this condition. We re-use per-problem II values from `profiling_output_bf16_imp_s025/` and `profiling_output_bf16_imp_s030/`.
45	
46	**Per-phase II_uniform(phase, s) from existing data**:
47	- II(execution, s025) ≈ 7.20 (median), II(transition, s025) ≈ 0.053
48	- II(execution, s030) ≈ 14.64 (median), II(transition, s030) ≈ 0.053
49	
50	### 3.2 Condition B — Oracle Phase-Aware Allocation
51	
52	Phase-dependent sparsity using **true quantile-based phase labels** (from attention sparsity during generation). This is an upper bound on mitigation performance — a real controller would have noisier phase estimates.
53	
54	**Allocation rule** (compute-matched):
55	
56	Given target sparsity s and phases with token counts (n_exec, n_reas, n_trans):
57	
58	```
59	s_exec  = s - δ           (reduce sparsity in high-interaction phase)
60	s_reas  = s               (keep reasoning phase unchanged)
61	s_trans = s + δ'           (increase sparsity in low-interaction phase)
62	
63	Compute match constraint:
64	  n_exec × s_exec + n_reas × s_reas + n_trans × s_trans = N_total × s
65	  → δ' = δ × (n_exec / n_trans)
66	```
67	
68	**δ selection**: Use δ = 0.10 (i.e., execution phase gets s-0.10, transition gets s+δ').
69	
70	| Target s | s_exec | s_reas | s_trans (approx, depends on phase sizes) |
71	|----------|--------|--------|------------------------------------------|
72	| 0.25 | 0.15 | 0.25 | ~0.35 |
73	| 0.30 | 0.20 | 0.30 | ~0.40 |
74	
75	Note: δ' is computed per-problem based on actual phase token counts to ensure exact compute match. s_trans is capped at 0.50 to avoid degenerate masking.
76	
77	**Implementation**: For each problem:
78	1. Generate trace (reuse existing generation from bf16 runs, or regenerate if needed)
79	2. Segment into phases (quantile-based, same as existing pipeline)
80	3. For each phase, generate importance-based masks at s_phase
81	4. Run compound forward with phase-specific masks, extract PPL on that phase's tokens
82	5. Compute per-phase II under oracle allocation
83	
84	### 3.3 Condition C — Heuristic Baseline (Token Position Ratio)
85	
86	Phase assignment using **normalized generation position** as a zero-cost proxy:
87	
88	```
89	phase_heuristic(token_pos) =
90	  "execution"  if token_pos / seq_len < 0.33
91	  "reasoning"  if 0.33 ≤ token_pos / seq_len < 0.67
92	  "transition" if token_pos / seq_len ≥ 0.67
93	```
94	
95	Same sparsity allocation rule as oracle, but applied based on positional phase labels. This is the simplest possible heuristic — it tests whether even crude position-based phase estimation captures enough signal for mitigation.
96	
97	**Why token position ratio**: (1) Zero compute cost — no attention weights needed. (2) Prior work (ThinKV) observes that reasoning models often follow a "thinking → calculating → summarizing" trajectory, correlating with token position. (3) Establishes a lower bound that any real phase detector must beat.
98	
99	**Alternative heuristic considered but deferred**: Cumulative attention entropy (requires attention weights, non-zero cost). If token position ratio shows >0% recovery, entropy-based heuristic becomes a natural follow-up.
100	
101	---
102	
103	## 4. Evaluation Metrics
104	
105	### 4.1 Primary: II Recovery Percentage
106	
107	For each problem i, sparsity level s, and phase p:
108	
109	```
110	II_uniform(i, s, p)  = II under Condition A
111	II_oracle(i, s, p)   = II under Condition B
112	II_heuristic(i, s, p) = II under Condition C
113	```
114	
115	**Aggregate metric** (per sparsity level):
116	
117	```
118	recovery_oracle(s) = 1 - median(II_weighted_oracle(i, s)) / median(II_weighted_uniform(i, s))
119	recovery_heuristic(s) = 1 - median(II_weighted_heuristic(i, s)) / median(II_weighted_uniform(i, s))
120	```
121	
122	Where `II_weighted(i, s) = Σ_p [n_p × II(i, s, p)] / N_total` is the token-count-weighted mean II across phases for problem i.
123	
124	### 4.2 Secondary: Per-Phase II Comparison
125	
126	Compare per-phase II between uniform and oracle:
127	- Execution phase: II should decrease (less sparsity → less compound interaction)
128	- Transition phase: II should stay low (already near zero, even with more sparsity)
129	- Net effect: overall weighted II decreases
130	
131	### 4.3 Gate Criterion
132	
133	```
134	PASS if: recovery_oracle(s025) > 30% OR recovery_oracle(s030) > 30%
135	FAIL otherwise → C5b = "future work"
136	```
137	
138	If heuristic also exceeds 30%, that's a bonus result but not required for the gate.
139	
140	### 4.4 Statistical Test
141	
142	Paired Wilcoxon signed-rank test on per-problem weighted II (uniform vs oracle, uniform vs heuristic). Report p-value and recovery percentage with bootstrap 95% CI (B=10000).
143	
144	---
145	
146	## 5. Implementation Plan
147	
148	### 5.1 Code Changes
149	
150	Create `run_mitigation_pilot.py` extending the existing profiling pipeline:
151	
152	```
153	pacs/profiling/run_mitigation_pilot.py
154	```
155	
156	**Key functions to add**:
157	
158	1. `compute_phase_aware_sparsity(target_s, delta, phase_token_counts) → Dict[str, float]`
159	   - Returns per-phase sparsity levels, compute-matched
160	   - Caps s_trans at 0.50
161	
162	2. `assign_heuristic_phases(seq_len, prompt_len) → List[Phase]`
163	   - Token position ratio → phase assignment
164	   - Returns Phase objects compatible with existing pipeline
165	
166	3. `run_mitigation_trial(model, tokenizer, problem, target_s, delta, phase_method) → Dict`
167	   - Runs one problem under one condition
168	   - phase_method ∈ {"oracle", "heuristic"}
169	   - Returns per-phase II values and weighted aggregate
170	
171	**Reuse from existing pipeline**:
172	- `load_model_and_tokenizer()` — unchanged
173	- `stratified_sample_math500()` — take first 30
174	- `InteractionIndexCalculator` — core II computation, unchanged
175	- `segment_by_quantile()` + `to_phase_annotation()` — oracle phase labels
176	- `_select_heads_to_mask()`, `_select_kv_to_evict()` — mask generation at arbitrary sparsity
177	- `compute_conditioned_perplexities()` — per-phase PPL computation
178	
179	**New logic** (~150 lines):
180	- Phase-aware sparsity allocation function
181	- Heuristic phase assignment function
182	- Main loop: for each problem × condition, generate phase-specific masks and compute II
183	- Output JSON with per-problem, per-condition II values
184	
185	### 5.2 Data Flow
186	
187	```
188	Input:
189	  - Existing bf16 generation traces (if saved) OR re-generate (adds ~30min)
190	  - Existing bf16 profiling results for uniform baseline comparison
191	
192	Pipeline (per problem):
193	  1. Generate trace → segment phases (oracle) / assign phases (heuristic)
194	  2. For each phase:
195	     a. Compute phase-specific sparsity level (compute-matched)
196	     b. Generate importance masks at phase-specific level
197	     c. Forward pass with phase masks, extract phase-token PPL
198	     d. Compute phase II
199	  3. Compute weighted aggregate II
200	  4. Compare against uniform baseline II
201	
202	Output:
203	  - mitigation_pilot_results.json:
204	    {config, per_problem: [{problem_id, uniform_ii, oracle_ii, heuristic_ii, recovery_pct, ...}]}
205	  - Summary statistics + gate evaluation
206	```
207	
208	### 5.3 Compatibility Notes
209	
210	- **Generation reuse**: If bf16 generation traces are saved in existing profiling output (check `profiling_results.json` for per-problem token sequences), reuse them. Otherwise re-generate — adds ~30min but ensures exact matching.
211	- **Mask generation**: `_select_heads_to_mask` and `_select_kv_to_evict` accept arbitrary sparsity levels. No changes needed.
212	- **Phase segmentation**: `segment_by_quantile` works on the sparsity trace from generation. Oracle uses this. Heuristic bypasses it.
213	
214	---
215	
216	## 6. GPU & Time Budget
217	
218	| Component | GPU Time | Notes |
219	|-----------|----------|-------|
220	| Model loading | 2 min | DeepSeek 7B bf16, single GPU |
221	| Generation (if needed) | 30 min | 30 problems × ~60s each |
222	| Oracle condition | 30 min | 30 problems × 2 sparsity × 3 phases × 3 forward passes |
223	| Heuristic condition | 30 min | Same as oracle |

230	
231	**Hard deadline**: Results needed by 05-22 for paper integration. With 1.5h runtime, scheduling is not a bottleneck — GPU availability is the sole constraint.

236

246	- Emphasize that even partial recovery validates the practical relevance of the phase-modulated severity finding
247	- Frame full optimization as future work
248	
249	### Scenario C: Oracle recovery < 10% (FAIL)
250	
251	- C5b = future work, one sentence in Discussion
252	- Possible explanations: (1) compound interaction at phase boundaries blurs the effect; (2) head pruning mask is global (can't be phase-specific); (3) KV eviction phase-awareness alone is insufficient
253	- Core claims C1-C6 unaffected — finding stands without mitigation
254	
255	### Risk: Generation trace mismatch
256	
257	If bf16 traces are not saved and re-generation produces different phase segmentations, uniform baseline II values will differ from existing n=50 data. Mitigation: always compare within the same run's generation (uniform vs oracle/heuristic computed from the same trace), never cross-run.
258	
259	---
260	
261	## 8. Output Artifacts
262	
263	| Artifact | Path | Description |
264	|----------|------|-------------|
265	| Results JSON | `artifacts/mitigation_pilot_results.json` | Per-problem raw data |
266	| Summary | `artifacts/mitigation_pilot_summary.md` | Gate evaluation + key numbers |
267	| Figure | `paper_figures/fig_mitigation_pilot.pdf` | Bar chart: uniform vs oracle vs heuristic |
268	
269	---
270	
271	## 9. Handoff Checklist for Coding Worker
272	
273	- [ ] Check if bf16 profiling output contains saved generation traces (token IDs + attention sparsity per step)
274	- [ ] Create `pacs/profiling/run_mitigation_pilot.py` with the three functions specified in §5.1
275	- [ ] Reuse `stratified_sample_math500(seed=42)[:30]` for consistent problem selection
276	- [ ] Use δ=0.10 for phase-aware allocation, with s_trans capped at 0.50
277	- [ ] Compute-match constraint must hold exactly per-problem (not averaged)
278	- [ ] Output format: JSON with per-problem breakdown + aggregate summary
279	- [ ] Register artifact after completion
280	- [ ] Run on single GPU, ~1.5h total wall time
281

      "content": "# C5b Mitigation Pilot — Experiment Design\n\n> Claim: Phase-aware sparsity allocation recovers compound degradation\n> Status: plan_011 | Priority: P1 | Depends on: plan_009, plan_010 (n=500 running)\n> Gate: >30% II recovery → full rollout; else → future work\n> Budget: ~2h on 1 GPU (RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB)\n\n---\n\n## 1. Objective & Hypotheses\n\n**Goal**: Demonstrate that phase-aware sparsity budget allocation — informed by the phase-modulated severity gradient (C1/C2) — can reduce compound interaction degradation compared to uniform allocation.\n\n**Hypotheses**:\n- **H1 (Oracle)**: When true phase labels are known, reallocating sparsity budget away from the execution phase (high compound interaction) toward the transition phase (low compound interaction) recovers >30% of compound interaction (measured by II reduction).\n- **H2 (Heuristic)**: A zero-cost heuristic (token position ratio) recovers some compound interaction, but less than oracle, establishing a lower bound.\n\n**Why this matters**: C1-C3 establish that compound interaction is phase-dependent. C5b closes the loop: if severity is phase-dependent, phase-aware allocation should mitigate it. Even a modest pilot result (1) validates the practical relevance of the finding, and (2) provides a concrete pointer for future work on phase-gated controllers.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Pilot Configuration\n\n| Parameter | Value | Rationale |\n|-----------|-------|-----------|\n| Model | DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B | Strongest signal (median_diff 3.97/9.94 at s025/s030) |\n| n_problems | 30 | Sufficient for paired test (existing stratified sample subset) |\n| Sparsity levels | s=0.25, s=0.30 | Largest compound interaction, most room for recovery |\n| Phases | 3 (execution/reasoning/transition) | Quantile-based segmentation, consistent with C1-C3 |\n| Bootstrap | B=10000 | Consistent with bf16 canonical data |\n\n**Why not Qwen**: Qwen's compound interaction is ~1/4 of DeepSeek's magnitude (2.619 vs 9.94 at s030). Smaller signal → harder to detect recovery. If oracle recovers >30% on DeepSeek, Qwen replication is a separate follow-up.\n\n**Why not s015/s020**: Small compound interaction (0.32/1.15) leaves little room for meaningful recovery. Risk of floor effects.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Experimental Conditions (per problem, per sparsity level)\n\n### 3.1 Condition A — Uniform Baseline (existing data)\n\nStandard importance-based compound sparsity at level s, applied uniformly across all phases.\n\nThis is the **existing bf16 importance data** (already collected). No new GPU compute needed for this condition. We re-use per-problem II values from `profiling_output_bf16_imp_s025/` and `profiling_output_bf16_imp_s030/`.\n\n**Per-phase II_uniform(phase, s) from existing data**:\n- II(execution, s025) ≈ 7.20 (median), II(transition, s025) ≈ 0.053\n- II(execution, s030) ≈ 14.64 (median), II(transition, s030) ≈ 0.053\n\n### 3.2 Condition B — Oracle Phase-Aware Allocation\n\nPhase-dependent sparsity using **true quantile-based phase labels** (from attention sparsity during generation). This is an upper bound on mitigation performance — a real controller would have noisier phase estimates.\n\n**Allocation rule** (compute-matched):\n\nGiven target sparsity s and phases with token counts (n_exec, n_reas, n_trans):\n\n```\ns_exec  = s - δ           (reduce sparsity in high-interaction phase)\ns_reas  = s               (keep reasoning phase unchanged)\ns_trans = s + δ'           (increase sparsity in low-interaction phase)\n\nCompute match constraint:\n  n_exec × s_exec + n_reas × s_reas + n_trans × s_trans = N_total × s\n  → δ' = δ × (n_exec / n_trans)\n```\n\n**δ selection**: Use δ = 0.10 (i.e., execution phase gets s-0.10, transition gets s+δ').\n\n| Target s | s_exec | s_reas | s_trans (approx, depends on phase sizes) |\n|----------|--------|--------|------------------------------------------|\n| 0.25 | 0.15 | 0.25 | ~0.35 |\n| 0.30 | 0.20 | 0.30 | ~0.40 |\n\nNote: δ' is computed per-problem based on actual phase token counts to ensure exact compute match. s_trans is capped at 0.50 to avoid degenerate masking.\n\n**Implementation**: For each problem:\n1. Generate trace (reuse existing generation from bf16 runs, or regenerate if needed)\n2. Segment into phases (quantile-based, same as existing pipeline)\n3. For each phase, generate importance-based masks at s_phase\n4. Run compound forward with phase-specific masks, extract PPL on that phase's tokens\n5. Compute per-phase II under oracle allocation\n\n### 3.3 Condition C — Heuristic Baseline (Token Position Ratio)\n\nPhase assignment using **normalized generation position** as a zero-cost proxy:\n\n```\nphase_heuristic(token_pos) =\n  \"execution\"  if token_pos / seq_len < 0.33\n  \"reasoning\"  if 0.33 ≤ token_pos / seq_len < 0.67\n  \"transition\" if token_pos / seq_len ≥ 0.67\n```\n\nSame sparsity allocation rule as oracle, but applied based on positional phase labels. This is the simplest possible heuristic — it tests whether even crude position-based phase estimation captures enough signal for mitigation.\n\n**Why token position ratio**: (1) Zero compute cost — no attention weights needed. (2) Prior work (ThinKV) observes that reasoning models often follow a \"thinking → calculating → summarizing\" trajectory, correlating with token position. (3) Establishes a lower bound that any real phase detector must beat.\n\n**Alternative heuristic considered but deferred**: Cumulative attention entropy (requires attention weights, non-zero cost). If token position ratio shows >0% recovery, entropy-based heuristic becomes a natural follow-up.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Evaluation Metrics\n\n### 4.1 Primary: II Recovery Percentage\n\nFor each problem i, sparsity level s, and phase p:\n\n```\nII_uniform(i, s, p)  = II under Condition A\nII_oracle(i, s, p)   = II under Condition B\nII_heuristic(i, s, p) = II under Condition C\n```\n\n**Aggregate metric** (per sparsity level):\n\n```\nrecovery_oracle(s) = 1 - median(II_weighted_oracle(i, s)) / median(II_weighted_uniform(i, s))\nrecovery_heuristic(s) = 1 - median(II_weighted_heuristic(i, s)) / median(II_weighted_uniform(i, s))\n```\n\nWhere `II_weighted(i, s) = Σ_p [n_p × II(i, s, p)] / N_total` is the token-count-weighted mean II across phases for problem i.\n\n### 4.2 Secondary: Per-Phase II Comparison\n\nCompare per-phase II between uniform and oracle:\n- Execution phase: II should decrease (less sparsity → less compound interaction)\n- Transition phase: II should stay low (already near zero, even with more sparsity)\n- Net effect: overall weighted II decreases\n\n### 4.3 Gate Criterion\n\n```\nPASS if: recovery_oracle(s025) > 30% OR recovery_oracle(s030) > 30%\nFAIL otherwise → C5b = \"future work\"\n```\n\nIf heuristic also exceeds 30%, that's a bonus result but not required for the gate.\n\n### 4.4 Statistical Test\n\nPaired Wilcoxon signed-rank test on per-problem weighted II (uniform vs oracle, uniform vs heuristic). Report p-value and recovery percentage with bootstrap 95% CI (B=10000).\n\n---\n\n## 5. Implementation Plan\n\n### 5.1 Code Changes\n\nCreate `run_mitigation_pilot.py` extending the existing profiling pipeline:\n\n```\npacs/profiling/run_mitigation_pilot.py\n```\n\n**Key functions to add**:\n\n1. `compute_phase_aware_sparsity(target_s, delta, phase_token_counts) → Dict[str, float]`\n   - Returns per-phase sparsity levels, compute-matched\n   - Caps s_trans at 0.50\n\n2. `assign_heuristic_phases(seq_len, prompt_len) → List[Phase]`\n   - Token position ratio → phase assignment\n   - Returns Phase objects compatible with existing pipeline\n\n3. `run_mitigation_trial(model, tokenizer, problem, target_s, delta, phase_method) → Dict`\n   - Runs one problem under one condition\n   - phase_method ∈ {\"oracle\", \"heuristic\"}\n   - Returns per-phase II values and weighted aggregate\n\n**Reuse from existing pipeline**:\n- `load_model_and_tokenizer()` — unchanged\n- `stratified_sample_math500()` — take first 30\n- `InteractionIndexCalculator` — core II computation, unchanged\n- `segment_by_quantile()` + `to_phase_annotation()` — oracle phase labels\n- `_select_heads_to_mask()`, `_select_kv_to_evict()` — mask generation at arbitrary sparsity\n- `compute_conditioned_perplexities()` — per-phase PPL computation\n\n**New logic** (~150 lines):\n- Phase-aware sparsity allocation function\n- Heuristic phase assignment function\n- Main loop: for each problem × condition, generate phase-specific masks and compute II\n- Output JSON with per-problem, per-condition II values\n\n### 5.2 Data Flow\n\n```\nInput:\n  - Existing bf16 generation traces (if saved) OR re-generate (adds ~30min)\n  - Existing bf16 profiling results for uniform baseline comparison\n\nPipeline (per problem):\n  1. Generate trace → segment phases (oracle) / assign phases (heuristic)\n  2. For each phase:\n     a. Compute phase-specific sparsity level (compute-matched)\n     b. Generate importance masks at phase-specific level\n     c. Forward pass with phase masks, extract phase-token PPL\n     d. Compute phase II\n  3. Compute weighted aggregate II\n  4. Compare against uniform baseline II\n\nOutput:\n  - mitigation_pilot_results.json:\n    {config, per_problem: [{problem_id, uniform_ii, oracle_ii, heuristic_ii, recovery_pct, ...}]}\n  - Summary statistics + gate evaluation\n```\n\n### 5.3 Compatibility Notes\n\n- **Generation reuse**: If bf16 generation traces are saved in existing profiling output (check `profiling_results.json` for per-problem token sequences), reuse them. Otherwise re-generate — adds ~30min but ensures exact matching.\n- **Mask generation**: `_select_heads_to_mask` and `_select_kv_to_evict` accept arbitrary sparsity levels. No changes needed.\n- **Phase segmentation**: `segment_by_quantile` works on the sparsity trace from generation. Oracle uses this. Heuristic bypasses it.\n\n---\n\n## 6. GPU & Time Budget\n\n| Component | GPU Time | Notes |\n|-----------|----------|-------|\n| Model loading | 2 min | DeepSeek 7B bf16, single GPU |\n| Generation (if needed) | 30 min | 30 problems × ~60s each |\n| Oracle condition | 30 min | 30 problems × 2 sparsity × 3 phases × 3 forward passes |\n| Heuristic condition | 30 min | Same as oracle |\n| Analysis + bootstrap | 5 min | B=10000 on CPU |\n| **Total** | **~1.5h** (with re-generation) or **~1h** (with trace reuse) |\n\n**GPU**: 1× RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB. Can run on any of GPU 0-3 after c2_qw_n500 frees up GPU 2 or 3.\n\n**Timeline dependency**: plan_009 (c2_n500_deepseek, GPU 0+1) and plan_010 (c2_qw_n500, GPU 2+3) are currently running (~22h each). D005\\n- id: D007\\n  date: 2026-05-13 20:20 UTC\\n  summary: M1 结果无效（KV bug + KDE 失败）：两路并行——P0 修复重跑 + 替代 segmentation 开发\\n  rationale: KV eviction 的 per-layer mask 交集 bug 导致全 NaN（P0），KDE 82% 样本找不到 tri-modal\\n    结构（P1）。但积极信号存在：execution phase 对 head pruning 敏感度 474× vs reasoning 14×，说明 token\\n    级结构性差异真实存在。决策：(A) P0 修复后重跑 M1 获取有效 II + 诊断数据，(B) 并行开发 GMM+BIC / quantile-based\\n    / multi-layer 替代 segmentation。先看重跑 II 是否在现有 3 phase 间有差异，差异显著则 KDE 虽粗糙但假设成立；无差异则上替代方案。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: mvp_interaction_index_profiling\\n    role: failed_run\\n    result: KV NaN + KDE 82% fallback + 90% transition\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D001\\n- id: D008\\n  date: 2026-05-13 21:23 UTC\\n  summary: M1v2 假设未获支持但测量方法有问题——M1v3 最后诊断：降 sparsity + quantile segmentation + NaN\\n    guard\\n  rationale: M1v2 所有 phase 均 super-additive、无 sign reversal，但 3 个方法论问题使结论不可靠：(1) 30%\\n    random masking 导致 24-33× PPL 退化，超出安全区；(2) KDE 90% fallback，phase 划分无意义；(3) NaN\\n    CI artifact 导致虚假 PASS。M1v3 修复这三点后做最终判定。叙事 pivot：即使全 super-additive，magnitude 跨\\n    segment 差异（+17.8 vs +3.1）仍支持 phase-aware allocation。Pass = magnitude CI 不重叠；Fail\\n    = magnitude 无差异。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: mvp_ii_profiling_v2\\n    role: diagnostic\\n    result: All super-additive, no sign reversal, NaN artifact PASS, KDE 90% fallback\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D007\\n- id: D009\\n  date: 2026-05-13 22:44 UTC\\n  summary: Random masking 系统性混淆——v1-v3 测了错误条件；importance-based masking 是正确 MVP 测试，立即启动\\n    v4\\n  rationale: 三次 MVP 实验全用 random masking（torch.randperm 选 heads/KV），天然产生 super-additive\\n    interaction（随机移除组件造成相关性损伤）。初步 importance-vs-random 对比已显示 masking 方法是 II 符号的主导因子（importance\\n    II=-0.91 sub-additive vs random II=+9.38 super-additive）。v3 在 clean methodology\\n    下仍全 super-additive 是预期结果，不代表 PACS 假设无效。真正的 MVP 判定需在 importance-based masking 条件下测试\\n    per-phase II——这才是实际部署条件下的 compound sparsity 行为。v3 high vs mid 在 s=0.2 下 CI 几乎不重叠（[1.06,3.55]\\n    vs [-1.06,1.30]），暗示 importance-based 下 magnitude 差异可能更显著。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: mvp_ii_profiling_v3\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: negative under random masking, but confirms methodology is sound\\n  - exp_id: mvp_ii_profiling_v2\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: negative, methodology issues identified and fixed in v3\\n  - exp_id: mvp_interaction_index_profiling\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: failed (bugs), but importance-vs-random preliminary data is key signal\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D008\\n- id: D010\\n  date: 2026-05-13 22:44 UTC\\n  summary: MVP FAIL on phase-dependent reversal; PIVOT to strategy-dependent direction\\n    reversal as core novelty\\n  rationale: 三轮 MVP 实验（v1 bug/v2 negative/v3 negative）确认 phase-dependent direction\\n    reversal 不存在——所有 phase 均 super-additive，v2 的 5.8x magnitude 差异是 KDE 不均衡 artifact。但\\n    importance vs random masking 数据发现 strategy-dependent direction reversal：random\\n    30% II=+9.38（super-additive）vs importance 30% II=-0.91（sub-additive），20% 也有符号反转。这是更干净、更强的\\n    novelty 锚点，直接 motivate learned compound controller。Pivot 方向：以 strategy-dependent\\n    interaction reversal 为核心 claim，phase conditioning 降为可选的二级贡献。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - D010\\n- id: D013\\n  date: 2026-05-14 01:16 UTC\\n  summary: 暂停 importance masking 结论，根因确认：cumulative_attention_received 有因果注意力位置偏差，驱逐最近上下文导致灾难性退化\\n  rationale: Reviewer 根因分析确认：causal attention 中 position 0 被 N-1 个 token 关注（累积最高），position\\n    N-1 仅被自身关注（累积最低）。topk(largest=False) 驱逐最低分 = 驱逐最新位置 = 破坏最近上下文。这解释了 importance\\n    eviction 比 random 差 26-60× 的现象。s025 的 II per-sample mean (+152.7) 与聚合计算 (-107.6)\\n    符号相反，CI 宽 455 单位，统计意义为零。D010 的 strategy-dependent direction reversal 可能完全是坏指标伪影。修复方向：position\\n    normalization importance[j] /= (seq_len - 1	# C5b Mitigation Pilot — Experiment Design
2	
3	> Claim: Phase-aware sparsity allocation recovers compound degradation
4	> Status: plan_011 | Priority: P1 | Depends on: plan_009, plan_010 (n=500 running)
5	> Gate: >30% II recovery → full rollout; else → future work
6	> Budget: ~2h on 1 GPU (RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB)
7	
8	---
9	
10	## 1. Objective & Hypotheses
11	
12	**Goal**: Demonstrate that phase-aware sparsity budget allocation — informed by the phase-modulated severity gradient (C1/C2) — can reduce compound interaction degradation compared to uniform allocation.
13	
14	**Hypotheses**:
15	- **H1 (Oracle)**: When true phase labels are known, reallocating sparsity budget away from the execution phase (high compound interaction) toward the transition phase (low compound interaction) recovers >30% of compound interaction (measured by II reduction).
16	- **H2 (Heuristic)**: A zero-cost heuristic (token position ratio) recovers some compound interaction, but less than oracle, establishing a lower bound.
17	
18	**Why this matters**: C1-C3 establish that compound interaction is phase-dependent. C5b closes the loop: if severity is phase-dependent, phase-aware allocation should mitigate it. Even a modest pilot result (1) validates the practical relevance of the finding, and (2) provides a concrete pointer for future work on phase-gated controllers.
19	
20	---
21	
22	## 2. Pilot Configuration
23	
24	| Parameter | Value | Rationale |
25	|-----------|-------|-----------|
26	| Model | DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B | Strongest signal (median_diff 3.97/9.94 at s025/s030) |
27	| n_problems | 30 | Sufficient for paired test (existing stratified sample subset) |
28	| Sparsity levels | s=0.25, s=0.30 | Largest compound interaction, most room for recovery |
29	| Phases | 3 (execution/reasoning/transition) | Quantile-based segmentation, consistent with C1-C3 |
30	| Bootstrap | B=10000 | Consistent with bf16 canonical data |
31	
32	**Why not Qwen**: Qwen's compound interaction is ~1/4 of DeepSeek's magnitude (2.619 vs 9.94 at s030). Smaller signal → harder to detect recovery. If oracle recovers >30% on DeepSeek, Qwen replication is a separate follow-up.
33	
34	**Why not s015/s020**: Small compound interaction (0.32/1.15) leaves little room for meaningful recovery. Risk of floor effects.
35	
36	---
37	
38	## 3. Experimental Conditions (per problem, per sparsity level)
39	
40	### 3.1 Condition A — Uniform Baseline (existing data)
41	
42	Standard importance-based compound sparsity at level s, applied uniformly across all phases.
43	
44	This is the **existing bf16 importance data** (already collected). No new GPU compute needed for this condition. We re-use per-problem II values from `profiling_output_bf16_imp_s025/` and `profiling_output_bf16_imp_s030/`.
45	
46	**Per-phase II_uniform(phase, s) from existing data**:
47	- II(execution, s025) ≈ 7.20 (median), II(transition, s025) ≈ 0.053
48	- II(execution, s030) ≈ 14.64 (median), II(transition, s030) ≈ 0.053
49	
50	### 3.2 Condition B — Oracle Phase-Aware Allocation
51	
52	Phase-dependent sparsity using **true quantile-based phase labels** (from attention sparsity during generation). This is an upper bound on mitigation performance — a real controller would have noisier phase estimates.
53	
54	**Allocation rule** (compute-matched):
55	
56	Given target sparsity s and phases with token counts (n_exec, n_reas, n_trans):
57	
58	```
59	s_exec  = s - δ           (reduce sparsity in high-interaction phase)
60	s_reas  = s               (keep reasoning phase unchanged)
61	s_trans = s + δ'           (increase sparsity in low-interaction phase)
62	
63	Compute match constraint:
64	  n_exec × s_exec + n_reas × s_reas + n_trans × s_trans = N_total × s
65	  → δ' = δ × (n_exec / n_trans)
66	```
67	
68	**δ selection**: Use δ = 0.10 (i.e., execution phase gets s-0.10, transition gets s+δ').
69	
70	| Target s | s_exec | s_reas | s_trans (approx, depends on phase sizes) |
71	|----------|--------|--------|------------------------------------------|
72	| 0.25 | 0.15 | 0.25 | ~0.35 |
73	| 0.30 | 0.20 | 0.30 | ~0.40 |
74	
75	Note: δ' is computed per-problem based on actual phase token counts to ensure exact compute match. s_trans is capped at 0.50 to avoid degenerate masking.
76	
77	**Implementation**: For each problem:
78	1. Generate trace (reuse existing generation from bf16 runs, or regenerate if needed)
79	2. Segment into phases (quantile-based, same as existing pipeline)
80	3. For each phase, generate importance-based masks at s_phase
81	4. Run compound forward with phase-specific masks, extract PPL on that phase's tokens
82	5. Compute per-phase II under oracle allocation
83	
84	### 3.3 Condition C — Heuristic Baseline (Token Position Ratio)
85	
86	Phase assignment using **normalized generation position** as a zero-cost proxy:
87	
88	```
89	phase_heuristic(token_pos) =
90	  "execution"  if token_pos / seq_len < 0.33
91	  "reasoning"  if 0.33 ≤ token_pos / seq_len < 0.67
92	  "transition" if token_pos / seq_len ≥ 0.67
93	```
94	
95	Same sparsity allocation rule as oracle, but applied based on positional phase labels. This is the simplest possible heuristic — it tests whether even crude position-based phase estimation captures enough signal for mitigation.
96	
97	**Why token position ratio**: (1) Zero compute cost — no attention weights needed. (2) Prior work (ThinKV) observes that reasoning models often follow a "thinking → calculating → summarizing" trajectory, correlating with token position. (3) Establishes a lower bound that any real phase detector must beat.
98	
99	**Alternative heuristic considered but deferred**: Cumulative attention entropy (requires attention weights, non-zero cost). If token position ratio shows >0% recovery, entropy-based heuristic becomes a natural follow-up.
100	
101	---
102	
103	## 4. Evaluation Metrics
104	
105	### 4.1 Primary: II Recovery Percentage
106	
107	For each problem i, sparsity level s, and phase p:
108	
109	```
110	II_uniform(i, s, p)  = II under Condition A
111	II_oracle(i, s, p)   = II under Condition B
112	II_heuristic(i, s, p) = II under Condition C
113	```
114	
115	**Aggregate metric** (per sparsity level):
116	
117	```
118	recovery_oracle(s) = 1 - median(II_weighted_oracle(i, s)) / median(II_weighted_uniform(i, s))
119	recovery_heuristic(s) = 1 - median(II_weighted_heuristic(i, s)) / median(II_weighted_uniform(i, s))
120	```
121	
122	Where `II_weighted(i, s) = Σ_p [n_p × II(i, s, p)] / N_total` is the token-count-weighted mean II across phases for problem i.
123	
124	### 4.2 Secondary: Per-Phase II Comparison
125	
126	Compare per-phase II between uniform and oracle:
127	- Execution phase: II should decrease (less sparsity → less compound interaction)
128	- Transition phase: II should stay low (already near zero, even with more sparsity)
129	- Net effect: overall weighted II decreases
130	
131	### 4.3 Gate Criterion
132	
133	```
134	PASS if: recovery_oracle(s025) > 30% OR recovery_oracle(s030) > 30%
135	FAIL otherwise → C5b = "future work"
136	```
137	
138	If heuristic also exceeds 30%, that's a bonus result but not required for the gate.
139	
140	### 4.4 Statistical Test
141	
142	Paired Wilcoxon signed-rank test on per-problem weighted II (uniform vs oracle, uniform vs heuristic). Report p-value and recovery percentage with bootstrap 95% CI (B=10000).
143	
144	---
145	
146	## 5. Implementation Plan
147	
148	### 5.1 Code Changes
149	
150	Create `run_mitigation_pilot.py` extending the existing profiling pipeline:
151	
152	```
153	pacs/profiling/run_mitigation_pilot.py
154	```
155	
156	**Key functions to add**:
157	
158	1. `compute_phase_aware_sparsity(target_s, delta, phase_token_counts) → Dict[str, float]`
159	   - Returns per-phase sparsity levels, compute-matched
160	   - Caps s_trans at 0.50
161	
162	2. `assign_heuristic_phases(seq_len, prompt_len) → List[Phase]`
163	   - Token position ratio → phase assignment
164	   - Returns Phase objects compatible with existing pipeline
165	
166	3. `run_mitigation_trial(model, tokenizer, problem, target_s, delta, phase_method) → Dict`
167	   - Runs one problem under one condition
168	   - phase_method ∈ {"oracle", "heuristic"}
169	   - Returns per-phase II values and weighted aggregate
170	
171	**Reuse from existing pipeline**:
172	- `load_model_and_tokenizer()` — unchanged
173	- `stratified_sample_math500()` — take first 30
174	- `InteractionIndexCalculator` — core II computation, unchanged
175	- `segment_by_quantile()` + `to_phase_annotation()` — oracle phase labels
176	- `_select_heads_to_mask()`, `_select_kv_to_evict()` — mask generation at arbitrary sparsity
177	- `compute_conditioned_perplexities()` — per-phase PPL computation
178	
179	**New logic** (~150 lines):
180	- Phase-aware sparsity allocation function
181	- Heuristic phase assignment function
182	- Main loop: for each problem × condition, generate phase-specific masks and compute II
183	- Output JSON with per-problem, per-condition II values
184	
185	### 5.2 Data Flow
186	
187	```
188	Input:
189	  - Existing bf16 generation traces (if saved) OR re-generate (adds ~30min)
190	  - Existing bf16 profiling results for uniform baseline comparison
191	
192	Pipeline (per problem):
193	  1. Generate trace → segment phases (oracle) / assign phases (heuristic)
194	  2. For each phase:
195	     a. Compute phase-specific sparsity level (compute-matched)
196	     b. Generate importance masks at phase-specific level
197	     c. Forward pass with phase masks, extract phase-token PPL
198	     d. Compute phase II
199	  3. Compute weighted aggregate II
200	  4. Compare against uniform baseline II
201	
202	Output:
203	  - mitigation_pilot_results.json:
204	    {config, per_problem: [{problem_id, uniform_ii, oracle_ii, heuristic_ii, recovery_pct, ...}]}
205	  - Summary statistics + gate evaluation
206	```
207	
208	### 5.3 Compatibility Notes
209	
210	- **Generation reuse**: If bf16 generation traces are saved in existing profiling output (check `profiling_results.json` for per-problem token sequences), reuse them. Otherwise re-generate — adds ~30min but ensures exact matching.
211	- **Mask generation**: `_select_heads_to_mask` and `_select_kv_to_evict` accept arbitrary sparsity levels. No changes needed.
212	- **Phase segmentation**: `segment_by_quantile` works on the sparsity trace from generation. Oracle uses this. Heuristic bypasses it.
213	
214	---
215	
216	## 6. GPU & Time Budget
217	
218	| Component | GPU Time | Notes |
219	|-----------|----------|-------|
220	| Model loading | 2 min | DeepSeek 7B bf16, single GPU |
221	| Generation (if needed) | 30 min | 30 problems × ~60s each |
222	| Oracle condition | 30 min | 30 problems × 2 sparsity × 3 phases × 3 forward passes |
223	| Heuristic condition | 30 min | Same as oracle |
224	| Analysis + bootstrap | 5 min | B=10000 on CPU |
225	| **Total** | **~1.5h** (with re-generation) or **~1h** (with trace reuse) |
226	
227	**GPU**: 1× RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB. Can run on any of GPU 0-3 after c2_qw_n500 frees up GPU 2 or 3.
228	
229	**Timeline dependency**: plan_009 (c2_n500_deepseek, GPU 0+1) and plan_010 (c2_qw_n500, GPU 2+3) are currently running (~22h each). Once any GPU frees up, this pilot can start immediately. Expected availability: ~05-15 12:00 CST (based on c2_qw_n500 progress at ~8/500 as of 05-14 22:00).
230	
231	**Hard deadline**: Results needed by 05-22 for paper integration. With 1.5h runtime, scheduling is not a bottleneck — GPU availability is the sole constraint.
232	
233	---
234	
235	## 7. Expected Outcomes & Contingencies
236	
237	### Scenario A: Oracle recovery > 30% (PASS)
238	
239	- C5b enters paper as "phase-aware allocation recovers X% of compound degradation (oracle upper bound)"
240	- If heuristic also recovers >10%, include both in paper as oracle/heuristic pair
241	- One paragraph in §5 Results + one figure (bar chart: uniform vs oracle vs heuristic II)
242	
243	### Scenario B: Oracle recovery 10-30% (MARGINAL)
244	
245	- C5b enters Discussion (§6) as "phase-aware allocation shows promising but modest recovery"
246	- Emphasize that even partial recovery validates the practical relevance of the phase-modulated severity finding
247	- Frame full optimization as future work
248	
249	### Scenario C: Oracle recovery < 10% (FAIL)
250	
251	- C5b = future work, one sentence in Discussion
252	- Possible explanations: (1) compound interaction at phase boundaries blurs the effect; (2) head pruning mask is global (can't be phase-specific); (3) KV eviction phase-awareness alone is insufficient
253	- Core claims C1-C6 unaffected — finding stands without mitigation
254	
255	### Risk: Generation trace mismatch
256	
257	If bf16 traces are not saved and re-generation produces different phase segmentations, uniform baseline II values will differ from existing n=50 data. Mitigation: always compare within the same run's generation (uniform vs oracle/heuristic computed from the same trace), never cross-run.
258	
259	---
260	
261	## 8. Output Artifacts
262	
263	| Artifact | Path | Description |
264	|----------|------|-------------|
265	| Results JSON | `artifacts/mitigation_pilot_results.json` | Per-problem raw data |
266	| Summary | `artifacts/mitigation_pilot_summary.md` | Gate evaluation + key numbers |
267	| Figure | `paper_figures/fig_mitigation_pilot.pdf` | Bar chart: uniform vs oracle vs heuristic |
268	
269	---
270	
271	## 9. Handoff Checklist for Coding Worker
272	
273	- [ ] Check if bf16 profiling output contains saved generation traces (token IDs + attention sparsity per step)
274	- [ ] Create `pacs/profiling/run_mitigation_pilot.py` with the three functions specified in §5.1
275	- [ ] Reuse `stratified_sample_math500(seed=42)[:30]` for consistent problem selection
276	- [ ] Use δ=0.10 for phase-aware allocation, with s_trans capped at 0.50
277	- [ ] Compute-match constraint must hold exactly per-problem (not averaged)
278	- [ ] Output format: JSON with per-problem breakdown + aggregate summary
279	- [ ] Register artifact after completion
280	- [ ] Run on single GPU, ~1.5h total wall time
281

Handoff Checklist for Coding Worker\n\n- [ ] Check if bf16 profiling output contains saved generation traces (token IDs + attention sparsity per step)\n- [ ] Create `pacs/profiling/run_mitigation_pilot.py` with the three functions specified in §5.1\n- [ ] Reuse `stratified_sample_math500(seed=42)[:30]` for consistent problem selection\n- [ ] Use δ=0.10 for phase-aware allocation, with s_trans capped at 0.50\n- [ ] Compute-match constraint must hold exactly per-problem (not averaged)\n- [ ] Output format: JSON with per-problem breakdown + aggregate summary\n- [ ] Register artifact after completion\n- [ ] Run on single GPU, ~1.5h total wall time\n",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have all the data I need to assess both the c3_llama fix and the c5b pilot design. Let me compile my findings.

**c3_llama fix assessment:**

1. ✅ sp_str bug fixed (Python instead of bc)
2. ✅ CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=2,3 correct
3. ✅ GPU resource compliance (cuda:2,3 within whitelist)
4. ✅ n=50 consistent with C3 pilot design
5. ✅ Progressing normally (8/50 at 22:48)


6. ⚠️ Missing --max_new_tokens 2048 and --min_phase_length 10 flags that were used in the c2_n500 scripts
7. ⚠️ There's a stuck pdflatex process consuming 100% CPU for over 130 minutes

**c5b pilot design assessment:**

The design looks solid overall — it's well-structured with a clear gate criterion of >30% II recovery, and the three experimental conditions (uniform, oracle, heuristic) provide good coverage. The compute-matched constraint is properly specified, and the token position ratio is accounted for.

I'm noticing several strengths: the existing bf16 data serves as an efficient uniform baseline, the bootstrap with B=10000 matches the canonical approach, and the paired Wilcoxon signed-rank test is the right choice. The δ=0.10 parameter creates meaningful contrast across the conditions, and the contingency planning covers all outcome scenarios with a reasonable budget of ~1.5 hours.

On the concerns side, using n=30 from the 50-problem stratified sample is acceptable for a paired Wilcoxon test to detect medium-to-large effects, though it might miss smaller ones. But since the gate criterion targets >30% recovery — which is a large effect — this sample size should be sufficient.

However, there's a more fundamental issue with the phase-specific implementation. The design mentions generating importance masks at the phase-specific level, but head pruning masks are inherently global across the entire model and can't be applied differently per phase within a single forward pass. KV eviction could potentially vary per phase, but the head pruning component of the compound sparsity strategy can't be phase-differentiated. This is a significant constraint that could limit how much recovery we actually achieve, and the design needs to clarify whether we're only applying phase-specific differentiation to the KV eviction part. I'm realizing the core issue: in a real system, you'd want different sparsity levels across different generation phases in a single forward pass, but the profiling pipeline measures phases separately with different masks applied globally. For autoregressive generation, you could theoretically adjust the head pruning mask and KV eviction at each token position, though I'm not sure the existing pipeline actually supports that kind of fine-grained control.

The design document outlines a workflow where you generate the sequence once, then run separate forward passes for each phase with phase-specific masks to measure PPL, which means calculating importance metrics and applying different sparsity levels per phase. The challenge is that when you combine head pruning and KV eviction simultaneously across phases, you need multiple forward passes and the II calculation becomes more involved—though the document does seem to anticipate this with its phase-specific mask generation and measurement steps.

There's a fundamental tension here: a forward pass that applies masks globally but only measures PPL on a subset of tokens doesn't truly implement phase-aware allocation, since the mask wouldn't actually change at phase boundaries within the pass itself. I should flag this as a design ambiguity.

The recovery metric using median of ratios is more robust than mean, but it can become unstable when II_uniform approaches zero, so using II_weighted instead would give a more stable aggregation across phases. For the s_trans cap at 0.50, with typical parameters like s=0.30 and δ=0.10, the transition sparsity would stay well below the cap, though extreme phase size ratios could cause the cap to bind and affect the exact compute balance.

Attention entropy was mentioned as a potential heuristic baseline in D052 but the design defers it for now, which is reasonable for a pilot. Looking at the timeline more carefully though—the current GPU progress suggests each GPU needs around 300 minutes for a single sparsity level, and with four levels per GPU plus c3_llama competing for GPU 2 and 3, the actual completion time would push closer to 18:00 CST on May 15 rather than the earlier estimate.

Checking against D052's requirements: the design uses 2 sparsity levels in about 1.5 hours which matches the "1-2 level, 2h pilot" directive, includes the required simple heuristic baseline with token position ratio, and implements exact per-problem compute matching as a hard constraint. All three compliance points check out. My main hesitation is around the implementation complexity of applying phase-specific masks during execution rather than just measuring them, and whether head pruning masks can realistically be phase-specific in practice.

Now I need to verify an important methodological detail about the c3_llama setup—specifically whether it's missing certain flags that the bf16 canonical experiments used.

```text
Now I can see that the bf16 canonical experiments (like `bf16_ds_imp_s035`, `bf16_qw_imp_s035`) DO use `--max_new_tokens 2048 --min_phase_length 10 --max_problems 50`. So these flags are part of the canonical experimental protocol.

The c3_llama fixed script does NOT use these flags. This is a methodological inconsistency — C3 results may not be directly comparable to C1/C2/C5 canonical data.

Specifically:
- Without `--max_new_tokens 2048`, the generation might use a different max length, potentially affecting phase detection and the number of phases


- Without `--min_phase_length 10`, short phases might be included that are excluded in canonical data

This is a warning-level issue. The experiment is still running early (8/50), so there's time to fix it by adding these flags to the script or restarting with them included.

I need to compile my findings for the Director — three main issues to report: the c3_llama fix verification showing the sp_str bug is resolved but missing canonical flags, the c5b pilot design which is well-structured but has an unresolved implementation question, and the stuck pdflatex process that's been running for over 130 minutes and should be terminated.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a thorough pilot design document. Let me now review it systematically for potential issues:

**Positive aspects:**
1. Clear gate criterion (>30% II recovery)
2. Compute-matched constraint — ensures fair comparison
3. Three conditions (uniform/oracle/heuristic) — good experimental design
4. Token position ratio as heuristic — zero-cost, principled choice
5. Reuse of existing bf16 data for uniform baseline
6. Contingency plans for all three outcomes
7. Reasonable GPU budget (~1.5h on 1 GPU)

**Issues I need to flag:**


There's a methodological concern with how head pruning masks interact with phase-specific allocation. The design assumes different sparsity levels can be applied per phase, which works fine for KV cache eviction since you can evict different fractions in each phase. But head pruning typically uses a global mask applied across the entire sequence, not phase-by-phase. If only KV eviction is phase-aware while head pruning stays uniform, the compound interaction metric might not show meaningful recovery gains since one component isn't actually phase-specific.

Looking at the interaction metric itself—II = PPL(both) - PPL(head_only) - PPL(kv_only) + PPL(none)—the phase-aware allocation changes sparsity per phase, meaning each phase gets evaluated under different sparsity levels. But if head pruning is global and only KV eviction varies by phase, you're really only changing one axis of the compound effect. The document even acknowledges this in Scenario C, noting that head pruning masks can't be phase-specific, so the pilot is fundamentally about phase-aware KV eviction sparsity while head pruning remains uniform across the sequence.

Re-reading the methodology more carefully, it says to generate importance-based masks at s_phase for each phase, which suggests both components should change per phase. However, in practice head pruning gets applied before generation starts as a fixed mask, while KV eviction happens dynamically during generation—so there's a fundamental mismatch between the conceptual design and the actual implementation that needs clarification.

There's also a concerning pattern in the oracle allocations: at s=0.25, the transition phase gets s_trans≈0.35, and at s=0.30 it jumps to s_trans≈0.40. Since transition phases typically have very few tokens in reasoning models, the compute-match constraint could push these sparsity levels even higher, and while there's a cap at 0.50 mentioned, δ=0.10 might be too aggressive for this regime.

The sample size of 30 problems is solid for a paired Wilcoxon test, and using the stratified sample with a fixed seed ensures reproducibility. But there's a critical issue with the generation trace reuse assumption: if head pruning masks change between conditions, the model will generate different tokens under different pruning regimes, meaning the oracle condition and uniform condition are actually operating on different token sequences entirely.

Wait, I need to reconsider this. The standard approach for importance-based evaluation is to generate the text once under the full model, then evaluate perplexity under various sparsity masks without regenerating. So the tokens stay fixed across all conditions, and the phase-aware allocation just determines which sparsity level applies to which tokens. That's the right design — single generation pass, then multiple evaluation passes with different phase masks applied to the same token sequence.

The timeline looks manageable — the current experiments should wrap up in another 12-16 hours, which leaves plenty of buffer before the deadline. The c5b pilot can start as soon as GPU capacity opens up.

But I'm still unclear on the mechanical details of phase-aware compound sparsity. The compute-matched constraint makes sense, but actually implementing different sparsity levels across phases requires figuring out how to apply different head pruning masks to different token groups. That's not straightforward in the current profiling pipeline, which applies a single uniform sparsity level. The pilot needs to handle phase identification, per-phase mask application, and potentially per-token masking if phases don't align cleanly with token boundaries.

Head pruning is the real bottleneck here — attention heads are shared across the entire sequence, so you can't just mask different heads for different positions without fundamentally changing the architecture. KV cache eviction is more tractable since you can vary eviction rates by phase. The design document glosses over this distinction, but it's a critical architectural constraint that needs explicit clarification.

I'm reconsidering the evaluation approach. The design mentions generating importance-based masks per phase, but the existing pipeline evaluates the full sequence with a single mask. For phase-aware evaluation, I'd need to generate the complete sequence first, then for each condition — uniform, oracle, heuristic — create phase-specific masks and measure their impact separately.

The tricky part is that this requires modifying the forward pass to apply different masks at different token positions, which is a non-trivial change from the current global mask application. The design estimates ~150 lines of new code for this, but that might be optimistic given the complexity of per-position masking.

Now I'm compiling my findings on the c3_llama fix and the overall state of the implementation.

For the c3_llama fix, the sp_str bug is resolved, GPU assignment looks correct with CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=2,3, and output directories are properly named. Skip logic is in place checking profiling_results.json, and GPU boundaries stay within the whitelist. However, there are missing flags like --max_new_tokens 2048 and --min_phase_length 10 that were used in the prior n=50 experiments, creating an inconsistency. The runs are progressing at 6/50 for both methods, and the registry has been updated correctly.

For the c5b pilot design, the experimental setup is sound with the compute-matched constraint and clear gate criterion, but I'm uncertain about how the phase-aware head pruning mechanism actually works since head masks are typically applied globally rather than per-phase. There's also a concern about the δ=0.10 threshold value.

Looking at the broader picture, there's a stuck pdflatex process that's been running for over 2 hours at full CPU, which needs attention, though all the c2 experiments are moving forward without issues. I should report these findings to the Director.

{
  "project": "phase-gated-compound-sparse",
  "text": "## 审查报告 — c5b_pilot_design (worker_prep_c5b-pilot-design)\n\n**结论**: WARNING — 设计总体合理，但有方法论缺陷需在编码前解决\n\n### 正面评价\n\n1. [severity: info] Gate criterion 明确（oracle >30% II recovery），三级 contingency（PASS/MARGINAL/FAIL）都有明确处理策略。\n2. [severity: info] Compute-matched constraint 确保公平比较——per-problem 级别的精确匹配。\n3. [severity: info] 三条件设计（uniform/oracle/heuristic）提供上下界，符合 D052 要求。\n4. [severity: info] 复用 bf16 现有数据作为 uniform baseline，节约 GPU 时间。\n5. [severity: info] GPU 预算合理（1.5h on 1 GPU），不是瓶颈。\n\n### 方法论问题\n\n6. [severity: warning] **Head pruning 的 phase-awareness 机制未明确**：设计中说\"For each phase, generate importance-based masks at s_phase\"，但标准 transformer 中 attention head mask 是 global 的（所有 token position 共用一套 mask），不能对不同 phase 的 token 应用不同 head mask。\n   - 如果仅 KV eviction 是 phase-aware 而 head pruning 仍为 uniform，则 compound interaction 只有一个轴被调节，recovery 效果可能有限。\n   - 如果要实现 per-phase head mask，需要修改 forward pass 在不同 token 位置切换 mask，这是非平凡的代码修改（不止 ~150 行）。\n   - **建议**：在 §3.2 明确说明 head pruning 是 global uniform 还是 per-phase。如果仅 KV phase-aware，论文需诚实报告\"仅调节 compound sparsity 的 KV eviction 维度\"。\n\n7. [severity: warning] **δ=0.10 在极端 phase 比例下可能导致 s_trans 过高**：公式 δ' = δ × (n_exec / n_trans)。如果 execution phase 占比远大于 transition phase（如 70% vs 10%），则 δ' = 0.10 × 7 = 0.70，加上 target s=0.30 则 s_trans=1.00。虽然有 0.50 cap，但 cap 后 compute-match 不再精确成立。\n   - **建议**：添加\"如果 cap 触发则按比例缩小 δ 以维持 compute match\"的逻辑。\n\n8. [severity: info] **n=30 统计功效可接受**：paired Wilcoxon at n=30 对 large effect 有足够 power。Gate criterion 是 30% recovery，属于 large effect。\n\n### 结论\n\n设计方向正确，但 §3.2 的 phase-aware 机制在 head pruning 维度上有概念模糊。建议编码 worker 开始前，Director 确认是仅 KV phase-aware 还是 compound（both heads + KV）phase-aware，因为这直接决定代码架构和预期效果量。"
}
